- finding-source · {"version":1,"source":"inv-4ebdbb23-9aba-4f61-ae91-b4a977ad8e63/cluster-8","artifact":"reviews/inv-4ebdbb23-9aba-4f61-ae91-b4a977ad8e63/final-review-inv-4ebdbb23-9aba-4f61-ae91-b4a977ad8e63.cluster-8.md","kind":"final-review","verdict":"not ready (a failed capability read mints a keyless seat; own-pane check ignores the host; subagent gate is dead on seat resolve)","occurrences":[{"section":"Must fix before landing","position":1,"key":"B1, `src/cli/site_capability.rs:50` with `src/client/local.rs:54-60` and `src/cli/hook.rs:2132`: a failed capability read is treated as \"not advertised\", so a keyed hook falls back to keyless enrollment and mints a second seat. `capabilities()` caches `Capabilities::none()` for any transport error for the client's lifetime, and its own comment says a caller that must tell a failed call from an answer uses `call_definitive(Command::Capabilities)`. The connect sub-budget is 2 s, so that one read can fail while later calls succeed. Failure trace: a clone relaunched in a new pane after un-retire, first Capabilities read times out; `reattach_and_install` returns `Declined` on the missing `SAME_SESSION_CONTINUITY_V1`; the pane is Unowned, so `check_in` runs `resolve_seat` with `key = None`; the daemon mints seat T while key K stays bound to unresolved S. Every later event then gets `pane_owned_by_other_seat`, and the clone has two identities until an operator acts. This contradicts spec 4.2 \"never a keyless fallback enrollment\" and TRUST-POLICY C1. Fix: when the environment carries a valid key, read the capability definitively and on a failed read end the hook quietly with no enrollment, keeping a no-capability answer as the only keyless path.","text":"B1, `src/cli/site_capability.rs:50` with `src/client/local.rs:54-60` and `src/cli/hook.rs:2132`: a failed capability read is treated as \"not advertised\", so a keyed hook falls back to keyless enrollment and mints a second seat. `capabilities()` caches `Capabilities::none()` for any transport error for the client's lifetime, and its own comment says a caller that must tell a failed call from an answer uses `call_definitive(Command::Capabilities)`. The connect sub-budget is 2 s, so that one read can fail while later calls succeed. Failure trace: a clone relaunched in a new pane after un-retire, first Capabilities read times out; `reattach_and_install` returns `Declined` on the missing `SAME_SESSION_CONTINUITY_V1`; the pane is Unowned, so `check_in` runs `resolve_seat` with `key = None`; the daemon mints seat T while key K stays bound to unresolved S. Every later event then gets `pane_owned_by_other_seat`, and the clone has two identities until an operator acts. This contradicts spec 4.2 \"never a keyless fallback enrollment\" and TRUST-POLICY C1. Fix: when the environment carries a valid key, read the capability definitively and on a failed read end the hook quietly with no enrollment, keeping a no-capability answer as the only keyless path."},{"section":"Must fix before landing","position":2,"key":"`src/cli/caller_location.rs:343`: `is_own_pane` compares only the pane id, not the Herdr server, so the key is presented for another server's pane with the same id, against spec 4.2 \"`ResolveSeat` presents the key only for the caller's own pane\". Failure trace: a keyed clone in `w1:p1` runs `--host-endpoint /other.sock seat resolve w1:p1`, or a recipient allocation for that pane (`src/cli/mod.rs:1315`, `src/cli/mod.rs:3924`). `recipient_host` picks the other server, so `key_decision` judges K at (other host, `w1:p1`) and returns `key_live_elsewhere`. The command is refused and a hold-bearing conflict row lands on an unrelated pane, where ordinary resolution stays refused and quiet in a hook until the row clears. With a reserved unbound key the same call binds K to the other server's seat. Pane ids like `w1:p1` collide naturally across servers. Fix: also require the resolve's host to be the caller's own server (`CallerEnv::pane_host` already spells it canonically), with a test.","text":"`src/cli/caller_location.rs:343`: `is_own_pane` compares only the pane id, not the Herdr server, so the key is presented for another server's pane with the same id, against spec 4.2 \"`ResolveSeat` presents the key only for the caller's own pane\". Failure trace: a keyed clone in `w1:p1` runs `--host-endpoint /other.sock seat resolve w1:p1`, or a recipient allocation for that pane (`src/cli/mod.rs:1315`, `src/cli/mod.rs:3924`). `recipient_host` picks the other server, so `key_decision` judges K at (other host, `w1:p1`) and returns `key_live_elsewhere`. The command is refused and a hold-bearing conflict row lands on an unrelated pane, where ordinary resolution stays refused and quiet in a hook until the row clears. With a reserved unbound key the same call binds K to the other server's seat. Pane ids like `w1:p1` collide naturally across servers. Fix: also require the resolve's host to be the caller's own server (`CallerEnv::pane_host` already spells it canonically), with a test."},{"section":"Must fix before landing","position":3,"key":"`src/cli/mod.rs:1315` with `src/cli/caller.rs:108` and `src/cli/mod.rs:1678`: the subagent gate is dead on `seat resolve`, so a subagent presents the parent's key, against TRUST-POLICY C1 \"only for a top-level, non-subagent caller\". The `ActiveGuard` lives inside `run_caller_scoped_deferred` and drops when that function returns `Ok(Some(parsed))`; `MutationSpec::Resolve` has `CallerNeed::None` and is handled afterwards at line 1306. `active()` is therefore `None` and `active_is_top_level()` always answers true there. Failure trace: a subagent runs `seat resolve <own pane>` and can bind a reserved key or write a conflict row and alert as if the top-level session had presented it. The direct harm is small, since spec 4.2 calls a same-pane child harmless, but the comment at `src/cli/site_capability.rs:55` is false on this path and the helper test passes the flag as a literal bool, so nothing catches it. Fix: compute the origin for this branch (`derive_refusing_origin` is already called at line 1066 and its result discarded) and pin it with a test that goes through the branch.","text":"`src/cli/mod.rs:1315` with `src/cli/caller.rs:108` and `src/cli/mod.rs:1678`: the subagent gate is dead on `seat resolve`, so a subagent presents the parent's key, against TRUST-POLICY C1 \"only for a top-level, non-subagent caller\". The `ActiveGuard` lives inside `run_caller_scoped_deferred` and drops when that function returns `Ok(Some(parsed))`; `MutationSpec::Resolve` has `CallerNeed::None` and is handled afterwards at line 1306. `active()` is therefore `None` and `active_is_top_level()` always answers true there. Failure trace: a subagent runs `seat resolve <own pane>` and can bind a reserved key or write a conflict row and alert as if the top-level session had presented it. The direct harm is small, since spec 4.2 calls a same-pane child harmless, but the comment at `src/cli/site_capability.rs:55` is false on this path and the helper test passes the flag as a literal bool, so nothing catches it. Fix: compute the origin for this branch (`derive_refusing_origin` is already called at line 1066 and its result discarded) and pin it with a test that goes through the branch."},{"section":"Untested scope","position":1,"key":"No test drives `seat resolve` (`src/cli/mod.rs:1315`), `allocate_recipient_seat` (`src/cli/mod.rs:3924`) or `me init` (`src/cli/me.rs:60`) with a key: coverage stops at the helper with literal bools and at `resolve_result` with a scripted reply, which is how the dead subagent gate went unseen.","text":"No test drives `seat resolve` (`src/cli/mod.rs:1315`), `allocate_recipient_seat` (`src/cli/mod.rs:3924`) or `me init` (`src/cli/me.rs:60`) with a key: coverage stops at the helper with literal bools and at `resolve_result` with a scripted reply, which is how the dead subagent gate went unseen."},{"section":"Untested scope","position":2,"key":"No test drives `enroll_pane` (Hermes SessionStart) with a key, neither its `ContinuityKeyPending` and conflict refusals nor `ServiceBusy`; the keyed hook tests take the lifecycle path through `check_in`.","text":"No test drives `enroll_pane` (Hermes SessionStart) with a key, neither its `ContinuityKeyPending` and conflict refusals nor `ServiceBusy`; the keyed hook tests take the lifecycle path through `check_in`."},{"section":"Untested scope","position":3,"key":"No test covers a Capabilities read that fails while later calls succeed, or a key presented with `--host-endpoint` naming another server.","text":"No test covers a Capabilities read that fails while later calls succeed, or a key presented with `--host-endpoint` naming another server."},{"section":"Untested scope","position":4,"key":"The plain CLI against a real daemon and a keyed hook through the real binary are outside this cluster's tests; `key_bound_elsewhere_mints_no_seat_on_plain_cli` uses a scripted reply. a fresh idle agent there gets no binding once the worker settles the claim as owned, until its next event."},{"section":"Untested scope","position":4,"key":"`seat resolve`, `me init` and recipient allocation with a real key are covered at the helper level (`site_capability.rs` tests, `on_own_server_tests`), not end to end through the binary for a key bound to another seat.","text":"`seat resolve`, `me init` and recipient allocation with a real key are covered at the helper level (`site_capability.rs` tests, `on_own_server_tests`), not end to end through the binary for a key bound to another seat."},{"section":"Deferred OK","position":1,"key":"On `PresentedKey::Unknown` the hook's `continuity_key` reads as no key, so `continuity_intent` supersedes a pending keyed intent and sends a keyless claim for that one event (`src/cli/hook.rs:2504`); enrollment still ends quietly and the next event reads again, but a fresh idle agent whose first capability read fails during lag keeps no key until a later event.","text":"On `PresentedKey::Unknown` the hook's `continuity_key` reads as no key, so `continuity_intent` supersedes a pending keyed intent and sends a keyless claim for that one event (`src/cli/hook.rs:2504`); this matches spec 4.2's top-level-only rule, and the path is unusual enough to leave.","text":"A subagent or refused-origin caller running `seat resolve` on its own unowned pane presents no key by design and can mint a keyless seat that a later keyed claim then meets as `pane_owned_by_other_seat`; this matches spec 4.2's top-level-only rule, and the path is unusual enough to leave."},{"section":"Deferred OK","position":3,"key":"`is_own_pane` compares against the environment's pane id, so a moved pane resolved under its new id through plain `seat resolve` or recipient allocation presents no key; the hook path is not affected because it sends the key with the current target.","text":"`is_own_pane` compares against the environment's pane id, so a moved pane resolved under its new id through plain `seat resolve` or recipient allocation presents no key; the hook path is not affected because it sends the key with the current target."},{"section":"Deferred OK","position":4,"key":"A keyed caller on a daemon without the capability makes a fresh `Capabilities` call on each `continuity_key` read, up to about four per event; this exists only in a mixed-version window between hook and daemon.","text":"A keyed caller on a daemon without the capability makes a fresh `Capabilities` call on each `continuity_key` read, up to about four per event; this exists only in a mixed-version window between hook and daemon."},{"section":"Deferred OK","position":5,"key":"Stale comments: the `reattach_by_continuity` doc in `src/cli/hook.rs` still says it runs for resume and for an event without a resolved pane, and the `ContinuityCheckIn` variant doc in `src/cli/journal.rs` still says resume-only.","text":"Stale comments: the `reattach_by_continuity` doc in `src/cli/hook.rs` still says it runs for resume and for an event without a resolved pane, and the `ContinuityCheckIn` variant doc in `src/cli/journal.rs` still says resume-only."}]}

the window is one pass, needs a seat with no row, and rebinds only to the seat's own saved target, and fencing the read on the published boot would close it."},{"section":"Deferred OK","position":4,"key":"Marker-held passes also hold back recording: a non-excludable refusal that keeps `pass_finished` false stops fingerprint recording for every seat on that host, so a handoff during that stretch leaves new seats unmatched; - finding-source · {"version":1,"source":"roast-code/round-1","artifact":"2026-11-06-graph-clone-identity-roast-pr-1.md","kind":"roast","verdict":"Should-fix (9 confirmed) [low coverage] [review readiness incomplete]","occurrences":[{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":1,"key":"[Should-fix] src/store/seats/reversible.rs:370","text":"[Should-fix] src/store/seats/reversible.rs:370 — The un-retire deadline shift for logical (unprojected) ACK receipts has untested branches: no test covers a recipient with `eligible_at_snapshot=0` (deadline read from `seat_availability`) and none covers a manifest under a `receipt_send_time_cutovers` row (`historical_decision_seq`); a wrong projection on those branches would survive the suite. [lanes: testing]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✗ REJECT-as-FYI / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: `shift_pending_deadlines` hand-copies the `effective_receipt_with_key_deadline` projection into the `receipt_state` insert (src/store/seats/reversible.rs:370-385). The only manual `prepared_recipients` insert in tests/store/reversible_retirement.rs:1108 uses `eligible_at_snapshot=1`. grep of tests/store/reversible*.rs, reversible_facts, reversible_session and facts_seat suites finds no `seat_availability`, `receipt_send_time_cutovers` or `eligible_at_snapshot=0`. Design §D2 (line 120) requires shifting logical `prepared_recipients` receipts. The reproduce seat compared the copied SQL against src/store/effective.rs:1833+ line by line and found it matches today (only an absent-cutover sentinel differs, -1 vs 0, on non-real sequences), so this is a test gap on duplicated logic, not a demonstrated defect.\n  severity note: kept at Should-fix: profile states a durable coordination store with data-integrity floors, and resumed ACK deadlines are durable data computed by duplicated SQL with no differential check.\n  fix-shape hint: add two reversible-retirement tests (an ACK-required send to a not-yet-eligible seat; a manifest under a `receipt_send_time_cutovers` row) that assert the shifted deadline equals `effective_receipt_with_key_deadline` plus the window, or replace the hand copy with a call to the shared projection."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":2,"key":"[Nit] src/store/seats/reversible.rs:412","text":"[Nit] src/store/seats/reversible.rs:412 — The home node and peers bound the un-retire deadline shift differently (`i64::MAX` at home, `payload.unretired_at` on peers and own-log replay), so the same obligation's copies can end up with different deadlines under origin clock skew; the code comment says the bound reads the same on every node. [lanes: correctness]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: `unretire` calls `unretire_local(..., i64::MAX)` (reversible.rs:412); `unretire_by_fact` passes `payload.unretired_at` on both the peer and own-log replay paths; `shift_pending_deadlines` filters on `m.decision_at <= created_by`. The doc comment \"the bound reads the same on every node\" and the line-411 comment \"no clock skew can leave one behind\" hold only at home. Design line 120 says home and peers \"shift by the same amount\". Needs an origin clock ahead of the home's with a pending row whose decision_at falls after unretired_at.\n  fix-shape hint: pass `unretired_at` as the bound in `unretire` too (so home and replay agree), or correct both comments to state the asymmetry and why it is accepted."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":3,"key":"[Nit] src/store/seats/cooperative.rs:1053","text":"[Nit] src/store/seats/cooperative.rs:1053 — Alert-only conflict rows (`pane_owned_by_other_seat`, `seat_holds_other_key`) are cleared only by a later check-in on that same pane; the design says they clear when the judged owner stops being the pane's resolved owner, but nothing does that on the owner's own transitions (unresolve, operator retire, reversible retire while the pane stays published). [lanes: correctness]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: `clear_alert_only_if_owner_changed` (src/store/continuity_keys.rs:370) has one caller, `register_available` at cooperative.rs:1053 with `Some(seat)`. Other cleaners are narrower: `clear_conflict_on_target` (operator.rs:297, 384), `clear_conflicts_absent_from` (observation.rs:2111), `clear_conflicts_of_key` (continuity_keys.rs:280). grep for `continuity_key_conflicts` outside continuity_keys.rs finds only kicks.rs:263. Design §4.2 line 91: \"Their row clears when `owner_seat_id` stops being the pane's resolved owner.\" Impact: stale `conflict_of_key` in `SeatInspect` and a slot held against the 256-row cap; `hold_kind` excludes alert-only kinds so no pane is ever blocked.\n  fix-shape hint: call `clear_alert_only_if_owner_changed`-style cleanup (by owner seat) from the paths that unresolve or retire a seat, or clear rows by `owner_seat_id` in `end_open_bindings`/retirement."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":4,"key":"[Nit] src/identity/repair.rs:687","text":"[Nit] src/identity/repair.rs:687 — `record_shell_fingerprints` reads the pane's shell pid with an unfenced host call (no expected boot/epoch) after listing gaps in a separate store read, so a Herdr restart landing between the listing and the read can record the new incarnation's shell under the seat's old (boot, incarnation, terminal) row and later let a cold restart match as Continuous. [lanes: concurrency-async, premortem, hot-path-perf]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: gaps listed under one writer turn (`shell_fingerprint_gaps`, src/store/seats/continuity.rs:1104, requires `s.structural_incarnation=g.incarnation`), turn dropped, then `self.host.pane_shell_pid(&gap.target, &self.quiet_read_context(&budget))`; `quiet_read_context` (repair.rs:858-870) sets `expected_boot: None, expected_epoch: None`; native `pane_shell_pid` (src/host/native.rs:1405) uses `run_unfenced`. `record_shell_fingerprint` (continuity.rs:1158) compares only DB columns, unchanged while reconciliation lags. After reconciliation `judge` (src/identity/shell_continuity.rs:38) returns Continuous on the live new shell, contradicting design line 48 \"A cold restart cannot match\" and TRUST-POLICY line 476. Window: at most the 750 ms process-info call.\n  fix-shape hint: pass the listing's snapshot boot/epoch into the read context (fenced read), or re-check the store's current incarnation against the gap inside the record turn before `INSERT OR REPLACE`."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":5,"key":"[Nit] tests/service/observation_lane.rs:1159","text":"[Nit] tests/service/observation_lane.rs:1159 — `a_decline_is_remembered_ten_minutes_and_gone_is_retried` jumps the fake clock by about 10 minutes while the observation lane it started is still running; nothing stops that lane from running its own pass (which calls `decide_shell_continuity` and bumps `shell_reads`) between the test's direct calls, so the comment \"no lane pass runs\" rests on timing only. [lanes: test-causality-lifecycle]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: `Rig::started_with_shell()` (observation_lane.rs:895-902) calls `rig.start()`; the test does `clock.0.fetch_add(10*60*1000 - 1)` with no `clock_advanced()`. `Pacer::wait_blocking` (src/service/pacer.rs:371-392) waits on the condvar with a real-time timeout of at most 5000 ms and then re-reads the fake clock; once `now >= deadline` it returns `Wake::Tick`. A pass after a completed tick calls `record_shell_fingerprints` and `decide_shell_continuity` (src/service/workers.rs:2554-2555), sharing the decline map and `shell_reads` counter. Needs a multi-second stall of the test thread (starved runner). Test is new on this branch.\n  fix-shape hint: build the rig without `start()` for this test (seed shell/process state directly), or cancel the lane before moving the clock; keep the same read counts."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":6,"key":"[Nit] tests/clone_identity_sweep.rs:945","text":"[Nit] tests/clone_identity_sweep.rs:945 — In `agent_exit_with_pane_open_keeps_the_seat`, the waits \"the keyed relaunch's binding\" and \"the keyless relaunch's binding\" (`open_bindings(&seat) != 0`) pass on the first probe because the first session's binding is still open, so the test never shows that either relaunch bound to the kept seat. [lanes: test-causality-lifecycle]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: `open_bindings` (clone_identity_sweep.rs:204-209) counts `occupant_bindings` rows with `ended_at IS NULL`, no liveness check. Codex `EndEvent` only disconnects the binding (src/harness/codex.rs:266); `bindings::disconnect` \"never ends a binding\" (src/store/bindings.rs:476-481). The `exit()` closure asserts the seat stays resolved with no retirement, so nothing ends the old binding. `seats()` unchanged and `key_seat == seat` still catch a relaunch that mints a new seat; a relaunch that is held or never binds would pass.\n  fix-shape hint: wait for an open binding whose `native_session` equals the session `start_agent` returned (or a connected binding newer than the pre-exit one), keeping the existing assertions."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":7,"key":"[Nit] tests/service/continuity_key_session.rs:142","text":"[Nit] tests/service/continuity_key_session.rs:142 — The new in-process daemon fixtures in continuity_key_session.rs and reversible_session.rs serialize only through the in-binary `IN_PROCESS_DAEMON` mutex; no nextest test group covers them, so under the full suite they run beside each other and beside the service-daemon chain the config says runs one at a time. [lanes: test-isolation-environment]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: continuity_key_session.rs:142 takes `super::IN_PROCESS_DAEMON`; reversible_session.rs reuses that fixture. The service-daemon override at .config/nextest.toml:203 filters on `binary(service) & test(/^(resolution|composition|operator|graph_d2)::/)`, leaving both modules out; this diff's nextest.toml change adds only the clone-identity-sweep group. AGENTS.md: \"A test that must not overlap others of its kind gets a nextest test group, not only an in-binary mutex.\" Each fixture has its own uuid temp root, so the only risk is CPU/wall-clock contention against latency-asserting tests. graph_send already has the same gap on base (noted as precedent, not pre-existing for these new modules).\n  fix-shape hint: add `continuity_key_session|reversible_session` to the service-daemon override regex and its exclusion counterpart."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":8,"key":"[Nit] docs/operations.md:286; docs/operations.md:290; docs/operations.md:249","text":"[Nit] docs/operations.md:286; docs/operations.md:290; docs/operations.md:249 — docs/operations.md was not updated for this diff: it still says live handoff does not recover launched agents that never checked in, its repair-history table omits the four new allocation decision kinds, and its retirement and restore-hold sections do not mention G5 absence retirement, reversible retirement or key-based reattachment. [lanes: hygiene-docs]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: `git diff --stat` shows docs/operations.md untouched; only docs/programmatic-service-client.md and TRUST-POLICY.md changed under docs. Line 286 at head contradicts commit 9d3090daa (shell-fingerprint continuity for launches that never checked in). The table at lines ~290-296 lists five kinds and omits `structural_shell_continuity`, `cooperative_continuity_key`, `service_unretire`, `service_finalize` (migration 0016 CHECK list). Lines 219 and 249 describe retirement and reattachment without G5, reversible retirement or key reattachment. grep of operations.md for absence/unretire/structural_shell finds nothing.\n  fix-shape hint: one doc pass on operations.md: update line 286, add the four kinds to the table, add a sentence each on G5 absence retirement, reversible retirement and key reattachment with a pointer to TRUST-POLICY."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":9,"key":"[Nit] src/identity/repair.rs:727","text":"[Nit] src/identity/repair.rs:727 — The paging of `decide_shell_continuity` is untested at any level: the 32-candidate cap, the `shell_decide_cursor` advance, the wrap back to the start and the early stop on an exhausted budget; the recording side has an equivalent test. [lanes: testing]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived (spot check, Should-fix) / ground ✓)\n  evidence: `decide_shell_continuity` (repair.rs:726-751) reads `shell_decide_cursor`, lists up to `SHELL_CONTINUITY_CANDIDATES_PER_PASS` (32, src/ports.rs:1970) with `s.id>?3 ... LIMIT ?4` (src/store/seats/continuity.rs:1221), advances per candidate, resets only on `walked_all`, breaks on exhausted budget. tests/store/shell_continuity.rs always passes `after: None`; its only PER_PASS assertion (line 441) is the record-side cap. tests/service/observation_lane.rs calls `decide_shell_continuity` with one or two seats (lines 1154-1250). No test seeds more than 32 candidates or a non-None cursor.\n  severity note: two of three seats rated Nit; the refute (spot) seat rated Should-fix. Set to Nit: the logic is a few lines, a read of it found no defect, and the failure mode (seats left unresolved on a handoff with >32 bare panes) is recoverable by the next pass or an operator.\n  fix-shape hint: a store-level test with 33+ candidates asserting two pages, cursor advance, wrap to None, and an exhausted-budget early stop, mirroring `an_unreadable_shell_cannot_starve_the_others_and_reads_are_capped`."},{"section":"Rejected (with reason)","position":1,"key":"src/store/seats/reversible.rs:436","text":"src/store/seats/reversible.rs:436 — UnretireSeat does not clear `continuity_key_conflicts`, leaving a stale `key_retired_reversibly` hold on the pane. Rejected 2-1 (ground CONFIRM Nit). Design §4.3 says UnretireSeat makes \"no hold changes\" and §4.2 line 91 lists the clearing paths (later successful claim, publication drops target, operator rebind/fresh seat, key release); after un-retire the keyed clone's next event gets Reattach and the C1 rebind clears the row. Residual: stale cause text if graph undo relaunches in a different pane while P stays published. The ground seat's evidence cites the same code the majority addressed; not material dissent."},{"section":"Rejected (with reason)","position":2,"key":"src/store/seats/reversible.rs:345","text":"src/store/seats/reversible.rs:345 — Un-retire shifts window-created obligations by the whole window. Rejected 3-0. Design line 120 specifies a uniform `unretired_at - retired_at` shift across all pending tables; line 128 says in-window receipts \"stay frozen with the rest\". Sends to a retired seat are refused on home, so only in-flight peer facts are affected, and the effect is extra time, not loss."},{"section":"Rejected (with reason)","position":3,"key":"src/cli/site_capability.rs:58; src/cli/hook.rs:1940; src/cli/site_capability.rs:70","text":"src/cli/site_capability.rs:58; src/cli/hook.rs:1940; src/cli/site_capability.rs:70 — Cached failed capability read maps a later fresh list naming the capability to `Unknown`, so a keyed hook racing daemon boot enrolls nothing quietly. Rejected 3-0. Spec §4.2 \"never a keyless fallback enrollment\"; doc comments on `continuity_key_presentation` and `enrollment_key` state the deferral; the next event (a new process) re-reads. Spot REJECT: one row per fingerprinted local seat, at most 32 candidates per ~5 s background pass; a few thousand in-memory row visits; an index would add write cost on every fingerprint upsert; no budget covers it."},{"section":"Unverified nits (spot-checked)","position":42,"key":"[FYI] .config/nextest.toml:20","text":"[FYI] .config/nextest.toml:20 — The `clone-identity-sweep` group's `max-threads = 2` is unmeasured and may lengthen group wall time (~45 s+) to protect neighbours. Spot REJECT: sibling real-daemon groups cap at 1 or 2 for the same stated reason (sync-daemons comment); 9 tests beside an 18-thread pool are unlikely on the 300 s critical path; the finding itself calls it speculative."},{"section":"Unverified nits (spot-checked)","position":43,"key":"[FYI] migrations/node/0016_graph_clone_identity.sql:1","text":"[FYI] migrations/node/0016_graph_clone_identity.sql:1 — The branch merges four separable behaviour changes (G2, D1, D2, G5), a service protocol version, one migration and new fact kinds as one unit. Spot REJECT: design line 130 \"every operation lands on this one branch and ships in one release\"; G5's reversible-vs-final choice depends on the key; the work was built and merged as separate task branches (ht-ma6i.1-.4), so review granularity exists at that level; a process preference, not a defect."}]}

- finding-source · {"version": 1, "source": "roast-code/round-2", "artifact": "2026-11-06-graph-clone-identity-roast-pr-2.md", "kind": "roast", "verdict": "Should-fix (2 confirmed) [low coverage] [review readiness incomplete]", "occurrences": [{"section": "Confirmed findings", "position": 1, "key": "[Should-fix] src/host/native.rs:1405; src/identity/repair.rs:706; src/identity/repair.rs:703; src/identity/repair.rs:704", "text": "[Should-fix] src/host/native.rs:1405; src/identity/repair.rs:706; src/identity/repair.rs:703; src/identity/repair.rs:704 \u2014 The ht-ma6i.36 fence on the shell fingerprint read does not close the Herdr-restart race on the native host: `NativeCli::pane_shell_pid` only runs `check_context` (connection epoch, never the boot) before an unwitnessed `run_unfenced` read with no post-read `check_boot`/`check_epoch`, the epoch does not move on a restart unless a fenced call fails, and the write-side re-check compares against the still-unreconciled publication, so a restarted server's shell can still be recorded under the old (boot, incarnation, terminal) row despite the new doc comment claiming otherwise. [fix-regression] (regressed, see iteration 1: prior Nit at src/identity/repair.rs:687, fixed incompletely by ba73dbcbb) [lanes: correctness, premortem, concurrency-async, regression]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce \u2713 / refute \u2717-survived / ground \u2713)\n  evidence: `pane_shell_pid` (src/host/native.rs:1405-1418) is `self.check_context(context)?` then `self.run_unfenced([\"pane\",\"process-info\",..])`; no `check_boot`, no post-read `check_epoch`, response not witnessed (`dispatch(.., witnessed=false, fenced=false, ..)`, native.rs:1003-1011). `check_context` (native.rs:2560-2568) is `if context.expected_boot.is_some() && context.expected_epoch != Some(self.epoch())`: the boot is a flag, never compared. The adapter epoch moves only via `fetch_add` at native.rs:463, 1196, 1209 (failed launch / fenced dispatch / after-parse check) or `resume_after_epoch` at daemon start, so a restart on the same socket leaves `self.epoch()` equal to the published `gap.host_epoch`. The write-side re-check (src/store/seats/continuity.rs:1183-1189, `g.host_boot=?11 AND g.epoch=?12 AND g.incarnation=?5`) joins the active publication, which stays pre-restart until the next observation pass; `record_shell_fingerprints` runs inline after `record_reconciliation_pass` (src/service/workers.rs:2546-2555), so nothing reconciles in between. Window: a restart landing after the pass capture and before or during any of up to 16 reads (SHELL_FINGERPRINT_READS_PER_PASS \u00d7 750 ms), with restored panes keeping their target ids. Outcome: new shell recorded under the old row; after reconciliation `judge` returns Continuous across a cold restart, against design line 48 (\"A cold restart cannot match\") and TRUST-POLICY line 476 (C1). The doc comment at repair.rs:686-689 (\"a Herdr restart between the listing and the read records nothing\") describes the test fake, not production: the fake `LaneHost::pane_shell_pid` (tests/service/observation_lane.rs:197-205) refuses on `boot.as_str() != self.boot`, which native does not.\n  severity note: three seats at Should-fix; kept. Profile states a durable coordination store with data-integrity floors, and this is a TRUST-POLICY C1 continuity decision. Not Blocking: the window is the tail of one pass, narrower than before the fence, and the misjudgment needs a restored pane at the same target id. Raised from the prior Nit because the fix now claims the window closed and ships a test that gives false assurance.\n  fix-shape hint: make the shell read witnessed and call `check_boot` against `expected_boot` (as `observe_current_target_for_archival` and the fenced readers do), or re-verify the server incarnation / `check_epoch` after the read before recording; then either soften the doc comment or make it true."}, {"section": "Confirmed findings", "position": 2, "key": "[Should-fix] tests/service/observation_lane.rs:197", "text": "[Should-fix] tests/service/observation_lane.rs:197 \u2014 The restart fence is tested only against a `LaneHost` fake that is stricter than the native host (it refuses on boot mismatch, native `check_context` does not), and the restart test moves boot and epoch together, so the suite never exercises the case the doc comment promises: a Herdr restart that leaves the native connection epoch unchanged. (new this iteration; coupled with the finding above) [lanes: testing]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce \u2713 / refute \u2717-survived / ground \u2713)\n  evidence: fake `pane_shell_pid` (tests/service/observation_lane.rs:197-205) refuses with `StaleHostObservation` when `context.expected_epoch != Some(self.epoch...) || boot.as_str() != self.boot...`; its doc (line 85) says it mirrors \"the native host's `check_context`\". Native `check_context` (src/host/native.rs:2560-2567) compares only the epoch. `a_restart_between_listing_and_read_records_nothing` (observation_lane.rs:~1309) sets `boot=\"host-2\"` and `epoch=2` together, so the epoch mismatch alone refuses and the boot comparison is never exercised on its own. `a_publication_moved_during_the_read_records_nothing` covers only the store-side re-check after publication. No test covers a restart with the epoch unchanged, which is the production path above.\n  severity note: refute and ground seats at Should-fix, reproduce seat at Nit. Kept at Should-fix: the test is the only evidence behind the repair.rs:686-689 claim on a C1 invariant in a store the profile gives data-integrity floors, and it validates a stronger fence than production has. Fixing the first finding properly closes this one at the same time.\n  fix-shape hint: make the fake mirror native (epoch-only refusal, or have native compare the boot too), and add a restart case that moves the boot while leaving the epoch at the listing's value; assert nothing is recorded."}, {"section": "Rejected (with reason)", "position": 1, "key": "none this iteration. The 14 rejections and the spot-rejected nits of iteration 1 stand unchanged; no scout re-surfaced any of them and no packet carries `previouslyRejected`.", "text": "none this iteration. The 14 rejections and the spot-rejected nits of iteration 1 stand unchanged; no scout re-surfaced any of them and no packet carries `previouslyRejected`."}]}

- finding-source · {"version": 1, "source": "roast-code/post-cap-audit", "artifact": "2026-11-06-graph-clone-identity-roast-pr-post-cap-audit.md", "kind": "roast", "verdict": "Should-fix (1 confirmed) [low coverage] [review readiness incomplete]", "occurrences": [{"section": "Confirmed findings", "position": 1, "key": "[Should-fix] src/store/seats/observation.rs:1443; src/store/seats/observation.rs:1506", "text": "[Should-fix] src/store/seats/observation.rs:1443; src/store/seats/observation.rs:1506 \u2014 The G5 claimed-pane rule's `terminal_claimed_elsewhere` predicate has no `ended_at IS NULL` filter on `occupant_bindings` and no non-retired seat-state filter on `seats`, so a departed seat's ended binding or a retired seat's retained structural proof makes an unresolved seat's shown target count as absent and retires it (finally, for a keyless seat) after the grace, where before the diff the same state only withheld a watch and kept waiting. [fix-regression] [lanes: correctness, regression]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce \u2713 / refute \u2717-survived / ground \u2713)\n  evidence: src/store/seats/observation.rs:1443-1457 at a345c61: `SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM seats WHERE id!=?1 AND host_id=?2 AND structural_terminal_id=?3 AND structural_incarnation=?4) OR EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM occupant_bindings WHERE seat_id!=?1 AND terminal_id=?3 AND incarnation=?4)`; no `ended_at IS NULL`, no `state!='retired'`, while other binding reads in the same file filter on `ended_at IS NULL` (lines 269, 1192, 1208, 1987). Lines 1506-1512 now use `own_target_shown = !terminal_claimed_elsewhere(..)`; a true result skips the `TargetShown`/`None` return and enters the absence count, which after RESTORED_PANE_GRACE_MS yields `TargetGone` and a `restored_target_absent` retirement. Ground seat: `retire_row` (src/store/seats/publication.rs ~640) updates only state, generation, retired_at and retired_seq, leaving `structural_terminal_id`; `end_local_seat` sets `ended_at` and deletes nothing (bindings.rs:1349, operator.rs:387), so a departed claimant's rows keep matching. Before the diff the same SQL lived only in the `claimed_elsewhere` closure that withheld a watch (fail-safe: a wrong answer cost a delay). The new TRUST-POLICY G5 *Absent* rationale (\"that pane is the other seat's\") and the amended accepted limit cover only a session whose refused claim enrolled a fresh seat in the pane, not a claimant that has since left; the parallel shell-continuity rule (TRUST-POLICY:490) excludes retired seats' rows. The new test `a_target_showing_another_seats_pane_counts_as_absent` inserts only `resolved` seats with open `connected` bindings, so the ended-binding and retired-seat cases are untested. Reproduce seat's sequence: s2 runs in terminal T at unresolved s1's saved target and exits; s1's Codex TUI session is resumed in T (no hook until its first turn, no kept C1 claim); every coherent pass counts T as claimed and s1 retires, finally if keyless, while its own session sits idle in a pane owned by nobody.\n  fix-shape hint: in the absence-path use only, filter the seats half on `state!='retired'` and the bindings half on `ended_at IS NULL` (keep the watch-withholding use as it was), and add one test each for an ended binding and a retired seat's retained proof; align the TRUST-POLICY G5 *Absent* text and accepted limit with the narrowed predicate in the same commit."}, {"section": "Rejected (with reason)", "position": 1, "key": "none this iteration. The prior iterations' rejections stand unchanged; no scout re-surfaced any of them and no packet carries `previouslyRejected`.", "text": "none this iteration.
